## Text Elements
Coastal Research Case Study ^iBJ88nVj

What the law firm does

A person gets hurt in a car accident that someone else caused. The injured person, the client, hires a personal injury law firm to sue the person at fault, the defendant. At the firm, an attorney leads the case and paralegals do the paperwork. Each firm gives its cases its own case number, and each case records the date of the accident, the state it happened in, and the type of incident (car crash, slip and fall, dog bite).

Before the firm decides how much to demand, it has to know the most money the defendant's insurance can pay. That number is the policy limit. Asking for $500k when the policy only covers $50k wastes months. So the firm pays a research company (this company, Coastal Research) to find out.

What Coastal Research does

A paralegal logs into the client portal and submits a research request for one of the firm's cases. The request includes the defendant's details: name, address, date of birth, vehicle plate if known. They also pick how fast they need it (standard or rush), and rush costs more. A firm can submit more than one request for the same case, for example when a second defendant turns up. But it shouldn't submit the same request twice while one is still open.

An internal admin sees new requests and assigns each one to a researcher. The researcher works on it, and the request moves through stages: submitted → in progress → completed. It can also be cancelled by the firm, or closed as no coverage found.

While researching, the researcher may find several insurance policies for one defendant: an auto policy, an umbrella policy, a homeowner's policy. For each policy found, they record:
- the insurance company
- the policy number
- the coverage type
- the limit amounts, which often come as a pair, like $25,000 per person and $50,000 per accident
- the dates the policy was active, because it only counts if the policy was active on the accident date

They may attach a document as proof, such as the declarations page. When the researcher is done, the firm gets notified and can see the results in the portal.

Paralegals at one firm must never see another firm's cases or results. Admins can see everything. Researchers only work on requests assigned to them. ^C1S1WKlZ

Entities ^beJ0lIly

- User -> denotes the users with Roles like Paralegal, Researcher, Admin
- Firm / Law Firm
- Insurance Policy
- Research/Request
- Defendent -> Not particularly sure since it seems uncessary as we might 
never need their info again for any purpose
